**Decision: the package boundary and three requested design choices are APPROVED. Implementation is not yet cleared; please make the following contract-precision corrections in the proposal first, then stop for a final exact-SHA check.**

### Decisions (§10)
1. **1.1 mechanism: APPROVED** — use the additive delta merged over the frozen 1.0 $defs by name. Specify the merge as a deterministic build/load operation: preserve the 1.0 root metadata, replace only explicitly named definitions, reject duplicate/unknown override names, and validate every resulting 1.1 reference. Do not mutate or rewrite the 1.0 file.
2. **Runtime advertising: APPROVED** — keep SUPPORTED_VERSIONS = ("1.0",) in WP3.1. A 1.1 request must continue to fail closed until a later reviewed package implements and serves it.
3. **Answer fields: APPROVED** — coverage required in 1.1; model optional until the model-adapter package. No 1.1 requirements may leak into 1.0 validation.

### Corrections required before implementation
1. **OpenAPI compatibility wording:** §3 says every 1.0 path and operation id is “identical”, but also adds a version header to every operation and points 1.1 responses at the 1.1 contract. Define the invariant precisely: preserve every 1.0 path and operationId; allow only the explicit additive version-negotiation parameter and version-appropriate schema references/response header. Define exactly how the selected version is echoed, including the /v1/version exception. Add tests for path/operationId parity and the permitted delta, not whole-operation identity.
2. **Locator conditional requirements:** require header and authenticity="claimed" for header_field; require attachment_id for attachment_part; require both sheet and cell for sheet_cell. Reject irrelevant locator fields where practical. Add negative tests for each missing required field. The current proposal only explicitly specifies the header authenticity condition.
3. **Coverage/limitation semantics:** the current rule makes every non-complete state require indexing_incomplete. That is too broad for partial answers caused by unsupported sources or failed items. Define the state-to-limitation mapping so indexing_incomplete is required for indexing, while partial requires a truthful limitation corresponding to its pending/failed/unsupported coverage. Add positive and negative schema tests for these cases.

### Scope and safety
The revised separation is correct: WP3.1 remains contract/schema/specification and traceability only. ## Comment 7051272332 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## WP3.1 proposal v3, your three corrections applied (reply to 7051242604): base `eb9af24`, head `975f3fc`

Verified on GitHub: `975f3fc` is on the branch, parent `eb9af24`, one file changed (`core/docs/STAGE3_WP3_1_PROPOSAL.md`, +37/-13), documentation only. Nothing implemented. D1 to D3 are recorded as approved in §10.

**Corrections (each marked `[v3]` in the document)**
1. **OpenAPI invariant and echo (§2, §3, §5).** The invariant is now: every 1.0 path and `operationId` is preserved; the only permitted differences are the optional `ApiVersion` request parameter, the response header `X-Atlas-Api-Version`, and retargeting schema refs to the 1.1 document. Echo, exactly: the header carries the version **applied** (`1.0` when absent or asked for 1.0; `1.1` only when negotiated). **`/v1/version` is the exception:** the discovery call is never negotiated or refused for its header, has neither the parameter nor the echo, and returns `{api_version, supported}` as in 1.0 (the runtime already answers it before reading the header). The 1.0 file and runtime get none of this. Tests are path and `operationId` parity (set equality) plus the permitted delta (normalise away exactly those additions, then compare), not whole-operation identity.
2. **Locator (§3).** `anyOf`: the exact 1.0 definition limited to its four 1.0 kinds (so every 1.0 locator stays valid and the new fields are refused on old kinds) plus three strict branches with `additionalProperties: false`: `header_field` requires `header` and `authenticity = claimed`; `attachment_part` requires `attachment_id` (optional `page` or `block`); `sheet_cell` requires `sheet` and `cell`; `quoted` only on `message_part`. A negative test for every missing required field and every irrelevant field.
3. **Coverage and limitations (§3.1).** `indexing_incomplete` if and only if `state = indexing`; `complete` requires empty `pending` and `failed`; `partial` requires something pending or failed, must not carry `indexing_incomplete`, and each class needs its truthful limitation (attachments → `attachment_pending`, unsupported source → `source_unsupported`, OS access → `os_access_missing`, not-yet-indexed or failed → `partial_work`). `pending.category` and `failed.reason_code` are now closed enumerations. Positive and negative tests for every row; `searched = total` for `complete` is not schema-expressible and is recorded as a semantic invariant for the implementing package.

**Your D1 request, made precise (§3):** `build_1_1(base, delta)` is a pure, deterministic operation: keeps the 1.0 root metadata; the delta lists `x-overrides` and `x-additions`; it raises on duplicate names, an unknown override, an addition colliding with a 1.0 name, a name in both lists, or a `$defs` entry in neither list; replaces only listed names; sorts keys; validates that every `$ref` resolves and every definition compiles; never mutates the base. Each failure mode has a test, as does determinism.

**One observation I did not decide (§2, §10 item 4):** in 1.0 the version header is **not** part of the signed string. For 1.1 I recommend the signed string includes the applied version and that a 1.1 client refuses a response whose echo differs from what it asked; in WP3.1 that is specification text only (enforcement belongs to the identity package). Your call.

**Decision: bind the negotiated API version into the signed request for API 1.1.** The 1.0 signing format remains byte-for-byte unchanged for compatibility. For a 1.1 request, the canonical signed message must include the literal applied/requested API version (1.1) as an additional unambiguous field. The client must reject a response whose X-Atlas-Api-Version is absent or differs from the version it requested. A request signed for 1.1 must not validate as 1.0 if the version header is stripped or changed. /v1/version remains the unversioned discovery operation and retains its existing 1.0 signing behaviour. This is contract/specification only in WP3.1; actual signature enforcement belongs to the separately reviewed identity package. Document the exact canonical field order and delimiter/encoding so there is no ambiguity.

Section 3's OpenAPI summary still says the negotiation header is defined for “every operation”; correct it to “every operation except /v1/version”, consistent with sections 2 and 5. The v3 mapping is materially better and is accepted in principle. In the proposal, clarify that a partial answer caused by a failed item or not_yet_indexed item must include partial_work, and that every non-empty pending category must carry its mapped limitation. Keep the indexing_incomplete iff state=indexing invariant. Ensure the schema/test plan rejects missing mapped limitations and rejects contradictory mappings.

## WP3.1 proposal v4 (reply to 7051300100): base `975f3fc`, head `f7ecc6f`

Verified on GitHub: `f7ecc6f` is on the branch, parent `975f3fc`, one file changed (`core/docs/STAGE3_WP3_1_PROPOSAL.md`, +15/-9), documentation only. Nothing implemented. Changes are marked `[v4]` in the document.

1. **Signing (decision applied, §2).** 1.0 is unchanged: `UTF-8(METHOD_UPPERCASE LF target LF timestamp LF nonce LF hex(SHA-256(body)))`, five fields, LF = 0x0A, no trailing newline. **1.1 canonical message: `UTF-8("1.1" LF METHOD_UPPERCASE LF target LF timestamp LF nonce LF hex(SHA-256(body)))`**: six fields, the literal ASCII `1.1` first, same delimiter, encoding and field formats, no trailing newline, no other field; any field containing CR or LF is refused. The verifier builds the message from the version it **applies**, so stripping or changing the header on a 1.1-signed request, or adding `1.1` to a 1.0-signed one, yields a different message and fails; a six-field message can never equal a five-field one because no field contains LF. A 1.1 client rejects a response whose `X-Atlas-Api-Version` is absent or differs from the version it requested. `/v1/version` keeps 1.0 signing, no version field, no echo. WP3.1 is specification only: the `SignedRequest` description in `openapi-1.1.yaml` and a "Signing" section of `CONTRACT_1_1.md` state the rule, with known-answer vectors; enforcement belongs to the identity package. I checked the formulas against the real `identity.canonical`: the 1.0 form has 4 LFs (five fields), the 1.1 form 5 (six fields), and they differ for the same request. The specification tests (§5) cover the vectors, 1.0 byte compatibility with `identity.canonical`, the downgrade cases and the CR/LF refusal, using a test-local reference function (no runtime change).
2. **OpenAPI wording (§3).** Corrected to "every operation except `/v1/version`" for both the negotiation parameter and the echo header, and the 1.1 security-scheme description carries the versioned signing rule; the frozen 1.0 file is not changed.
3. **Coverage semantics (§3.1, §5).** Every non-empty `pending` category and a non-empty `failed` must carry its mapped limitation, no exceptions; a `partial` answer caused by a failed item or a `not_yet_indexed` item must include `partial_work`; `indexing_incomplete` iff `state = indexing` is unchanged. Contradictory mappings are rejected: `attachment_pending`, `source_unsupported` and `os_access_missing` are allowed only with their pending category and never with `complete`. I left `partial_work` without a converse because Stage 2 already uses it for other partial work (for example a retry that ended early); say if you want it constrained too. Negative tests are listed for each missing and each contradictory mapping.

**CI:** I report no CI result for `f7ecc6f` (not complete when I looked). Stopping for your final review.

- `docs/genesis/STAGE-3-CONTRACT-DELTA.md` is explicitly superseded; `core/docs/CONTRACT_1_1.md` is authoritative. The old routes/event/descriptor/coverage proposal must not be implemented.
- `docs/genesis/HANDOVER.md` and `STAGE-3-EXECUTION-PLAN.md` now reflect the actual WP3.1 gate.
- Root `README.md` was inspected and does not contain the stale Stage 3 status claim; the stale statement was in `docs/genesis/README.md`.

**Claude: please implement and test the three still-unresolved review findings below on this same branch and PR #8. Do not open another PR or start WP3.2.**

1. **Trusted per-item sensitivity (policy.py / gate.py / evidence boundary).** The current token correctly carries a grant ceiling, but `passage.get("sensitivity")` is only safe if that label is derived from trusted, immutable source metadata—not supplied by the retriever/content. Trace the complete descriptor path. Carry a trusted `SourceDescriptor.sensitivity` (or equivalent authenticated manifest field) with each passage and enforce the current grant's `max_sensitivity` before retrieval output is accepted, before context construction, and at the model boundary. Add adversarial tests for a high-sensitivity item mislabeled as medium by an untrusted retriever, and for a grant ceiling being lowered/replaced between token mint and use. If the contract cannot support this without a versioned change, stop and explain the smallest required contract delta; do not silently weaken the boundary.

2. **Revocation epoch/current grant revalidation (policy.py / gate.py).** The code now calls `PolicyEngine.revalidate` before each source read and at `GatedModel.generate`, which is good, but the gate currently only uses the refreshed permitted set for source-type revocation. Make the decision binding include current grant identity/scope/ceiling (or a monotonic revocation/version epoch) and verify it at every source-read boundary and immediately before model invocation. A token minted before revoke/regrant or scope change must not remain valid merely because the source type is still allowed. Add deterministic revoke-during-retrieval and revoke/regrant-before-model tests; assert the model receives zero protected passages.

3. **Atomic terminal state/event invariant.** The current service delegates to `TaskStore.transition(... events=...)`, and the store now commits state plus events in one transaction. Verify every terminal path (`completed`, `partially_completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `expired`, denial, recovery) uses this atomic path; identify any remaining append-after-transition path. Add fault-injection tests proving rollback leaves neither a terminal state without its corresponding terminal event nor an event without its state transition.

For each item: make the smallest coherent change; add regression tests that fail if the fix is reverted; run targeted tests, full Core tests on supported Python versions, lint/type checks, and mutation checks where practical. Report exact commit SHA, changed paths, commands/results, and remaining Mac-only limitations. Do not resolve review threads until the exact new head demonstrates the fix. Preserve the single-open-PR rule and keep PR #8 draft/unmerged pending independent review.

## Comment 7052190048 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## Three Stage 2 review findings implemented: head `43c127a` (base `d7bfaf9`), stopped for your exact-SHA review

Verified on GitHub: `43c127a` is on the branch, parent `d7bfaf9`. One commit, 15 files, `core/` only (no `docs/genesis/`, no WP3.2). **No contract change was needed:** the frozen 1.0 files are byte-identical (sha256 `65f5a3f6…` and `c653a427…`), and `SourceDescriptor`, `PassageRef` and the 1.0 answer routes already support these fixes.

### 1. Trusted per-item sensitivity (`gate.py`, `policy.py`, `evidence.py`)
**Descriptor path traced.** The label used to come from `passage.get("sensitivity")`, which the retriever supplies (so a retriever could understate it), and a missing label meant "covered". Now: `StoredSource.sensitivity` is a trusted, store-side label (None means the type's default from `policy-rules.json`); a new `gate.SourceLabels(SourceStore)` resolves a passage's label **only from the store by `source_id`**; any sensitivity on the passage is ignored. An item whose source is unknown to the store, whose type disagrees with the store, or whose label is missing or unknown has no label and `PermitToken.allows_item` never covers it (excluded and disclosed as `sensitive_items_excluded`). The same lookup is enforced at retrieval output (`GatedRetrieval`), context construction (`ContextBuilder`) and the model door (`GatedModel`); all three now require the labels object.
**Tests:** a retriever that mislabels a high item as low, medium or unlabelled is not believed (the model never receives it); a retriever that overstates is ignored too; unknown source, type confusion and an unknown label string fail closed; a forged passage label never reaches the context; the grant ceiling lowered in place between mint and use ends the authority (see 2).

### 2. Grant-bound tokens (`policy.py`, `gate.py`)
`PermitToken` now records, per source type, the **grant identity and ceiling it was minted under** (it replaces the mint-time ceiling map). `PolicyEngine.revalidate` keeps a type only while the same grant with the same ceiling still allows it; a revoke followed by a new grant, or a changed ceiling, reports `grant_revoked` for that type (no new reason code, so the 1.1 vocabulary is unchanged), and an old token gains nothing from a broader or newer grant. `revalidate` runs immediately before **each source read** and at the **model door**, as before.
**Tests:** revoke and regrant, token level (a fresh decision would be allowed, the old token is not; the retriever is not called; the model is not called); revoke and regrant between two reads; revoke and regrant after retrieval and before the model (the model receives zero passages, the answer is the stopped one, documents are reported withheld); a ceiling lowered in place during retrieval; a second broader grant leaves an unchanged first grant valid but gives the old token nothing; revoking the first while the broader remains ends it.

### 3. Atomic terminal state (`tasks.py`, `service.py`)
**Audit result.** Every terminal path (`completed`, `partially_completed`, `failed` by denial, model failure and citation failure, `cancelled` immediate and at a boundary, `expired`, recovery into needs-attention) already went through `TaskStore.transition(events=...)` or the cancel transaction. Three remaining gaps, now closed: (a) `TaskStore.append_event` was an unused public writer of an event outside any transition: **removed**; (b) `transition` accepted a terminal state with **any** events: it now **refuses a terminal transition unless it carries exactly one closing event** (`final` or `error`); (c) answer handling: the denial's safe answer was saved in its own transaction before the state changed, and `GET /answer` served any saved envelope, **including the checkpoint envelope of a task that had not finished**. Now the answer commits in the same transaction as the terminal state (`transition(..., answer=...)`) for the denial path, and `TaskService.answer` returns `conflict` (409, already documented for that route in 1.0) until the task is terminal. The immediate-cancel path now fires the same two crash hooks so it can be tested.
**Tests:** nine terminal paths × two crash points inside the transaction (after the state, after the events): each leaves neither a terminal state nor a half-written event nor a served answer, and after restart ends with exactly one closing event, a contiguous sequence and the closing event last; the store's refusal of a terminal transition with no, a non-closing, two or mixed closing events; a denied task's safe answer is not saved on its own; an unfinished task serves no answer through the service; a source scan that the service has exactly one way to change state.

### Results (developer-reported, this tree)
- `ruff check .` and `mypy src` clean. `core`: **431 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 396; the sensitivity tests of round 2 were rewritten to use the store label). Other suites, same command: `kit` 165 (with `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701.
- **Mutation checks, 12 of 12 caught** (exact-string, restored after each): retrieval believing the retriever's label; the type-disagreement check removed; no ceiling comparison; a missing or unknown label covered; `revalidate` ignoring grant identity and ceiling; comparing identity but not ceiling; a terminal transition accepted without a closing event; state committed before its events; an append outside a state change restored; an unfinished task serving its saved answer; the denial's answer saved on its own; the transition dropping the answer. (Two first attempts did not apply because of formatting and were redone against the current text.)
- Docs updated: `THREAT_REVIEW.md` (the two affected rows rewritten), `TRACEABILITY.md`, test counts.

### Limitations and decisions to know about
- **The real ingestion must write `StoredSource.sensitivity` itself** and never copy it from content or a retriever; Stage 3's source adapters (WP3.2) carry that obligation. The frozen 1.0 `PassageRef.sensitivity` stays accepted and is ignored.
- **Strict by design:** any change of grant identity or ceiling ends a running task as `denied`, rather than filtering items; the owner can start a new task.
- **Behaviour change inside the frozen contract:** `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/answer` for an unfinished task is now 409 `conflict` (1.0 already lists 409 for that route); it used to return a saved checkpoint envelope.
- `gate.GatedRetrieval`, `ContextBuilder` and `GatedModel` now take a `SourceLabels` argument (internal API of the Core, not the 1.0 contract).
- Not verified: anything Mac-only (Keychain, `LOCAL_PEERCRED`), real-kill and power-loss recovery (crashes are simulated by exceptions at transaction hooks). **CI:** I report no CI result for `43c127a` yet.

I did not resolve any review thread and did not open another PR. Stopping for your exact-SHA review; WP3.2 not started.

## Comment 7052271330 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## Independent review blocker — PR #8 head `43c127a`: resume does not bind checkpointed data to its original grant

I reviewed the exact current head `43c127a88c8ff44bf927707980ba9cc6681b7a91`. The implementation closes the in-flight token case, but I do not see the equivalent protection across a crash/restart.

**Code path:** `TaskService._run()` obtains a fresh `decision, token = self._token(...)` after loading the saved checkpoint. `_revalidate()` then derives `touched` from saved passage/citation source types and checks only `token.allows(t, now)`. The checkpoint data written by `_retrieve()` contains passages and excluded count, but no authorising permit ID or per-source grant ID/ceiling. `PermitToken.grants` records the grant identity/ceiling only for the newly minted token.

**Why this matters:** if a task retrieves protected data, the process crashes, the original grant is revoked, and a new grant for the same source type is issued before recovery, the fresh token can allow that source type and the old checkpoint passages/draft can be reused. A lower ceiling on a replacement grant can likewise allow checkpointed passages to be reused without checking their trusted per-item sensitivity against the current ceiling. The existing in-flight revoke/regrant tests do not establish the crash/recovery case.

**Required fix (fail closed):**
1. Persist the authorising permit/grant identity and ceiling with the checkpointed material when it is first retrieved (and preserve that binding through later checkpoint updates).
2. On recovery, require the same grant identity and ceiling for every source type represented in saved passages/citations/answer; a revoke/regrant or changed ceiling must invalidate the old material. Do not silently authorise old data under a newly issued grant.
3. Re-resolve each saved passage's sensitivity from `SourceStore` via `SourceLabels` and check it against the current grant ceiling before any saved passage, draft, or verified answer can be reused or returned. Unknown/missing source labels must fail closed.
4. Add regression tests that crash after retrieval and after drafting, then revoke/regrant the same source type before recovery; also lower the ceiling before recovery. Assert old protected content reaches neither the model nor the final answer. Include a case where a replacement grant is broader, to prove it cannot inherit old checkpoint data.

Please implement this on PR #8 only, report the exact commit, and stop for an exact-SHA review. Keep WP3.2 unstarted and do not merge.

Source: https://github.com/Prasanna666666/Prasanna/pull/8#issuecomment-7052271330

## Resume/checkpoint grant binding — fix for the blocker on `43c127a`

**Commit: `bd62fc1af38cad052b49618b6f015e872e22d514`** (single commit on PR #8, pushed, origin head verified). WP3.2 not started; PR not merged.

**What changed (`core/src/atlas_core/service.py`)**
1. `_retrieve()` saves `grant_binding` (per source type: grant id, ceiling, from the minted token) in the same checkpoint transition as the passages. Later checkpoint updates (draft, verification) merge into it, so it is preserved.
2. New `_valid_types()`: a source type's saved material is usable only if the current token carries the **same grant id and ceiling** as the binding, **and** every saved passage of that type has a trusted label re-read from `SourceStore` via `SourceLabels` that the current ceiling covers (`allows_item`). A missing binding, unknown source or missing label fails closed.
3. `_revalidate()` (resume) now uses it instead of `token.allows`, over passages, citations and the verified answer, so any failure ends the task through the existing denied path (nothing reused). `answer()` and `events_after()` use the same check, so a finished answer is also withheld after a regrant or lower ceiling. A broader replacement grant inherits nothing.
4. Checkpoints written before this change have no binding and are never reused (safe by design).

**Tests (+22, core now 453)**, `test_review_round3.py` section 4: crash after retrieval / drafting / verification × revoke-and-regrant at the same, a broader, and a narrower ceiling; ceiling lowered in place; saved passage relabelled above the ceiling; source label missing; binding missing (also asserts the binding survives the draft checkpoint); finished answer after regrant/lowered ceiling. They assert the model receives no old passages (no extra calls), and the old content (sentinel and the figure) is in neither the answer nor the event stream. Control: unchanged grant still resumes and completes.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each confirmed to fail tests, then restored):** identity/ceiling comparison removed → 12 fail; label coverage removed → 4 fail; both comparisons relaxed → 12 fail; binding not saved → 9 fail.

## Architect execution brief — WP3.2 Document-only vertical path (begin now)

WP3.1 is now **ACCEPTED for its specification/contract scope** by ChatGPT's exact-SHA review on `800d13743f6cf4ce0ab87b4b8e098d441e459a56` (review ID 6000401465). The current PR head is `bd62fc1af38cad052b49618b6f015e872e22d514`; PR #8 remains the only open PR, draft and unmerged. Stage 3 is authorised under G-027. Proceed with WP3.2 only; do not wait for another owner prompt.

### Objective
Build the first Mac-independent, synthetic-testable **Documents → policy gate → progressive index/retrieval → exact evidence/citation** vertical path in `core/`. It must be usable by a future macOS client through the existing versioned Core boundary, but do not claim an installable Mac app or real-source access in this package.

### Required work
1. Re-read the canonical PRD, `TARGET_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `STAGE-3-EXECUTION-PLAN.md` WP3.2, the Stage 3 baseline audit, `core/docs/CONTRACT_1_1.md`, and `core/docs/STAGE3_TRACEABILITY.md` at the current PR head. The 1.1 contract is specification-only; do not accidentally serve 1.1 or change frozen 1.0.
2. Audit `kit`/`library` extraction and indexing candidates before reuse. Reuse only bounded, deterministic components that pass provenance, parser-isolation, data-ownership and restart checks. Do not transplant a legacy app or treat its existing RAG path as trusted.
3. Implement a Core-owned document source boundary and synthetic source store/adapter. Documents must be independently default-deny. Enforce the current grant and trusted per-item sensitivity before content enters retrieval, cache, context or model. Revoke/regrant/ceiling changes must invalidate indexed and cached passages; no stale material can be cited or replayed.
4. Implement bounded parser/extractor interfaces for the explicitly supported, audited document formats only. Preserve source ID, version/integrity hash and exact page/section/block offsets through extraction, chunking, retrieval and citations. Unsupported, encrypted, corrupt, oversized, symlink/traversal and partially extracted files must become explicit needs-attention/coverage outcomes; never silently skip, truncate, guess or cite them.
5. Implement model-free tier-1 indexing and progressive newest-first usefulness. File ordering uses filesystem modification time; retain content-created dates separately and never silently substitute them. Missing timestamps use deterministic stable fallback and are counted in coverage. Show searched/total/pending/unsupported/failed coverage; indexing cannot be represented as complete until all invariants hold.
6. Make indexing asynchronous, bounded and restart-safe with durable checkpoints, idempotency, cancellation, disk-pressure handling and explicit failure. Interactive requests must not be starved by ingestion.
7. Integrate retrieval with the existing Core `Retriever`/`GatedRetrieval`/`ContextBuilder`/`GatedModel`/evidence path rather than adding a parallel policy bypass. Exact citations must resolve to the exact current source version and locator; changed/deleted sources fail closed.
8. Synthetic fixtures only in CI. Add adversarial tests for prompt injection in documents, parser bombs, symlinks/traversal, sensitivity understatement, stale/revoked grants and caches, changed/deleted sources, malformed locators, restart at every index boundary, idempotent retries, cancellation, unsupported formats, partial coverage, and redaction sentinels.
9. Update `core/docs/STAGE3_TRACEABILITY.md`, test plan, threat review, package documentation and relevant canonical status notes so each requirement is mapped to implementation, test and evidence class. Distinguish CI-verified from Mac-pending.

### Hard boundaries
- No Mail access or Mail attachment parsing in WP3.2. No Finance/Health real-data access.
- No real owner documents, no private data in CI/artifacts, no cloud inference, no private-data egress, no external side effects.
- No Swift/Xcode/Keychain/TCC/MLX/signing/notarisation claims without a Mac. Keep those explicit as pending.
- No destructive migration, legacy-store consolidation, app retirement, API 1.0 reinterpretation, or unapproved API changes. If an additive contract change is genuinely required, stop and report the smallest delta before implementing it.
- Do not begin WP3.3/WP3.4/WP3.5 as a shortcut. Do not open another PR. One review round = one commit.

Stop after one commit and report exact full SHA, parent, changed paths, design/legacy reuse decisions, test commands/results across the whole repo (`core`, `kit` including `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`, `library`, `finance`, `atlas`, `images`), CI run URLs/results, mutation/adversarial coverage, known limitations, rollback and explicit Mac-only pending evidence. ## WP3.2 Document-only vertical path — review checkpoint

**Commit: `818948bb062cac244c1af1ae2d707e230a00be39`** (single WP3.2 commit; parent `5b86a517f32f6fcaa80d2fb93398ac3843a31e2c`, your docs reconciliation, which I rebased onto after a non-fast-forward push; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. WP3.3/3.4/3.5 not started.

**Changed paths (9, all under `core/`):** new `src/atlas_core/documents.py`, `tests/test_documents.py` (44 tests), `docs/WP3_2_DOCUMENTS.md`; updated `docs/STAGE3_TRACEABILITY.md` (T2–T5, T10, T12 now name real tests), `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`; `tests/test_architecture.py` (layer entry for `documents`, layer 4); `tests/test_tasks.py` (the "only the task store opens a database" guard now allows exactly `documents.py` + `tasks.py`; both open their own new file with a caller-supplied key and touch no existing database). No `docs/genesis` edits, no contract change (frozen 1.0 untouched; 1.1 not served; no additive delta was needed).

**Design (details in `core/docs/WP3_2_DOCUMENTS.md`)**
- `Scanner` → bounded stdlib `Extractor` → encrypted SQLite `DocumentIndex`, which is both the `Retriever` and the `SourceStore` handed to the existing `TaskService`. No parallel policy: grant, trusted label (set by the index, never by file content) and the grant-bound checkpoint from `bd62fc1` apply unchanged.
- Exact evidence: one passage = one block, locator `{"kind":"block","block":n}`, exact span, `version_sha256` of the indexed text (valid against the frozen 1.0 `EvidenceRef`). `get()` re-hashes the bytes on disk on every resolution: changed, deleted, replaced-by-link or unreadable files are unresolvable, and old citations resolve to `stale`/`unresolvable`.
- Explicit coverage outcomes (reason codes only): `unsupported_format`, `corrupt` (bad UTF-8, NUL, nothing extractable), `oversized` (bytes or block count; refused, not truncated), `symlink_refused`. `coverage()` reports total/searched/pending/unsupported/failed/missing_timestamps/reasons/scan_complete/paused/complete; `complete` only if a scan finished uncut, and nothing is pending, unsupported, failed or paused.
- Newest first by file mtime; a document's own `created:` date is stored separately and never substituted; no usable timestamp → ordered last, counted.
- Restart-safe: per-document atomic commit, scan as one transaction after a durable `running` marker, idempotent re-runs. Bounded slices (`run(max_docs)`), cancellation, disk-pressure pause (nothing failed or lost). `purge()` deletes all indexed text on revocation; the index file is encrypted.

**Supported formats: plain UTF-8 text and Markdown only.** PDF/Office/HTML/OCR/encrypted documents are reported `unsupported_format` (never skipped or guessed); they need a separately reviewed, isolated parser (Mac/later package).

**Legacy reuse decision (audited, nothing reused):** `kit/rag.py` (own schema, no exact offsets/version hash), `library/readers.py` (shells out to tesseract/poppler, heavy engines, no isolation), `kit/extract.py` (model-driven finance extraction): none passes provenance/parser-isolation/data-ownership checks. Reasons are in the doc.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container)**
- `core`: **497 passed** on Python 3.13 and on Python 3.11 (was 453); includes `test_architecture.py`. ruff format/check and mypy clean.
- `kit` 165 (incl. `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`, re-run after the rebase), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701: all passed.
- CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the result separately (not claimed here).

**Adversarial/mutation coverage:** prompt injection in a document (only the granted type is searched; question untouched); parser bomb and oversized; symlink file, symlink directory, link in the middle of a path, traversal/absolute/NUL paths; file swapped for a link after indexing; a document claiming its own sensitivity vs the index label; sensitivity above the grant excluded and disclosed; changed/deleted source and stale citation; changed while the model is answering (not verified or cited); revoke + regrant between retrieval and recovery; purge then regrant starts empty; no grant → nothing searched; malformed locator/span; encrypted-at-rest check; log redaction of text, file name and path; six-boundary crash matrix converging to the clean-run index with no duplicates; idempotency; cancellation; disk pressure; slice bounds. Exact-string mutations of `documents.py` (12): each made tests fail except two. One survivor exposed a real gap (a link in the *middle* of a path), so I added a test and re-ran: now fails as it should. The other, removing the size check inside `read()`, is an equivalent mutant (the extractor refuses oversized input anyway).

**Known limitations (honest):**
1. Coverage is produced and tested but **not yet carried in the answer envelope**; that belongs with the 1.1 coverage fields (specification only, not served).
2. Tier-1 ranking is lexical term overlap; ranking quality and scale are unmeasured.
3. Source designation and the consent flow are later packages; the index takes its root and label from its constructor.
4. When every passage is excluded the existing service still calls the model with an empty context (pre-existing Stage 2 behaviour, unchanged here); flagging for your call rather than altering the pipeline in this package.
5. Canonical status notes in `docs/genesis` (HANDOVER/README/plan "WP3.2 not started") are yours; I did not edit them.

**Mac-only pending (not claimed):** TCC/FileVault folder access, real volumes, real power-loss, Keychain key provider (WP3.6), target-Mac performance, OS-level parser isolation, Swift/Xcode, MLX, notarisation.

## Comment 7052873707 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## WP3.2 corrections for review 6000541597 — one focused commit

**Commit: `ad6e59bcb6fb5d6124ac56ea419f8330dc913e4c`** (parent `818948bb062cac244c1af1ae2d707e230a00be39`; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. API 1.0 frozen, 1.1 specification-only, no scope broadening (no PDF/Office/Mail/Mac work).

**Blocker 1 — unreadable directories vanished from coverage: fixed** (`documents.py`)
- A directory that cannot be opened or listed is now a failed item `unreadable_directory`; an entry that cannot be examined is `unreadable_entry`; a pipe/device is `unsupported_entry` (never opened). Nothing is dropped, so `complete` stays false. Coverage carries only reason codes and counts; no path or content is logged or exposed.
- Tests: nested unreadable directory (coverage `failed=1`, `reasons={"unreadable_directory":1}`, `complete=False`, nothing from it searchable, no path in coverage; after the permission is fixed a rescan inspects it and `complete=True`); a failing listing plus a failing per-entry stat; a FIFO (would block forever if opened).

**Blocker 2 — symlink path-swap race: fixed**
- `Scanner` now opens the root, then every component relative to the already-open parent descriptor (`O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` for folders; `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` for the file, then an `fstat` regular-file check). The walk lists through the directory descriptor, so it too cannot follow a swapped link. The scanner refuses to construct where `dir_fd`/`O_NOFOLLOW`/`O_DIRECTORY`/`scandir(fd)` are not supported (fail closed).
- Testable seam `Scanner.seam(when, component)` runs the tree change at exact moments. Tests: folder swapped for a link **after** it was opened → still reads the in-root file (never the outside one); swapped **just before** → refused; file swapped for a link just before its open → refused; a link swapped in during the **walk** → reported `unreadable_directory`, outside folder not listed; file swapped for a pipe after indexing → refused without blocking; unsupported platform refuses to run. The earlier static symlink/traversal tests are kept.

**Required end-to-end correction — no usable passages: fixed** (`service.py`, `answer.py`)
- If retrieval yields zero usable passages (no match, or all excluded by sensitivity) the model is **not called**. The task checkpoints a fixed draft ("No usable source was found for this question, so no answer was produced."), no citations, verification `unverified`, limitation `partial_work` (plus `sensitive_items_excluded` when applicable), and ends `partially_completed`. ## G-O19 source designation and consent gate — review checkpoint

**Commit: `dcb0fe24c0cf05b9310da6f12d4b736b38532bfa`** (one implementation commit; parent `480443755aa00960a93dd1916894304a4c28344c`, your WP3.2-acceptance docs, which I rebased onto after a non-fast-forward push; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. **No Mail data is read, listed, indexed or opened; there is no Mail adapter.** WP3.4/3.5 not started; API 1.0 frozen; 1.1 not served; no new dependency; no `docs/genesis` edits.

**Changed paths (all `core/`):** new `src/atlas_core/designation.py`, `src/atlas_core/access.py`, `tests/test_designation.py` (57 tests), `docs/G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`; changed `src/atlas_core/documents.py` (gate), `tests/test_documents.py`, `tests/test_architecture.py` (layer entries; `api`/`transport` may not import `designation`/`access`/`documents`), `tests/test_tasks.py` (store allow-list now names the three separate files), `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/STAGE3_TRACEABILITY.md` (T6), `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`.

**How the brief's scope items are met**
1. **Versioned, content-free record and lifecycle:** not_configured → enabled | declined; declined → enabled; enabled → revoked; revoked → enabled with a NEW grant identity. Each change commits with a hash-chained audit row (types, states, grant id, reason code only; never folder names, content, prompts or secrets; tested).
2. **Durable grant store behind `GrantSource`:** `DesignationStore` (own encrypted file) issues unique opaque grant ids (valid `SourceGrant`, frozen 1.0), ceiling = the policy's own sensitivity for the type, purpose `answer_question`, optional expiry; revoke/regrant gives a new identity, so the existing token and checkpoint binding rejects old material. Fail closed: the whole record is re-verified on every use (chain, head/rollback, state vs audit, each grant's digest and revoked flag, schema version). 12 tamper/downgrade cases (rewritten/removed audit rows, raised ceiling, cleared revoked flag, forged grant, forced state, rewound head, schema 0, dropped table…) each make every grant disappear and every change raise `StoreCorrupt`.
3. **One onboarding decision:** `OnboardingDecision(enable, decline, expires_at)` (also from the 1.1 `ConsentRequest` shape). Validated whole and applied atomically; a refused decision changes nothing; Documents-only, Mail-only, neither, declined, partial choices are tested; nothing enables by default; one type never enables another; no per-folder model; request ids are single-use (an old enable cannot be replayed after a withdrawal). Attachments, Finance and Health are not designable.
4. **OS-permission provider interface:** `OSPermissionProvider.current(type) → PermissionEvidence`; Core accepts only fresh (≤30 s, not future-dated), positive evidence for exactly that type, asked at every check. Cached consent, a flag, UI state or an env var is never accepted (tests: absent, denied, unknown, stale, future-dated, wrong type, provider failure). `FakeOSPermissions` is the explicit deterministic CI provider. **No macOS/TCC behaviour is implemented or claimed.**
5. **Everything gated on both:** `SourceAccess` is the `GrantSource` the unchanged `PolicyEngine` consumes (no parallel policy). `DocumentIndex` now **requires** an `AccessCheck` (no default; `AllowAll` is test-only): scan, each document of an indexing run, search (raises `PermissionRevoked`) and `get()` (returns nothing) touch no stored text and no folder byte unless the owner grant and the OS evidence are both current (matrix over consent × OS state asserts zero scanner touches). Model context and model door remain gated by the unchanged `ContextBuilder`/`GatedModel` re-validation. Revocation (owner withdrawal, expiry, or a once-granted OS permission positively reported missing) revokes at once and runs the registered purge; a failed or never-run purge stays durably pending, keeps the source revoked, blocks a new grant (`PurgePending`) and is finished by `run_pending_purges()` at start. "Unknown" never revokes; only a positive loss after an earlier grant does.
6. **Owner-facing shapes internally:** `SourceAccess.consent_state()` returns the 1.1 `ConsentState` shape (validated in tests); no route is served; signed-client identity untouched.
7. **Tests named in the brief:** default deny on fresh install and restart; Documents-only/Mail-only/neither; OS absent/denied/revoked; consent withdrawn; expiry; corrupt store; revoke/regrant identity mismatch; revocation between scan/read/model (mid-run between documents, at search, and during the model call, with the derived index purged and no old content served); no source bytes touched before both gates pass; backward compatibility (policy decisions identical to `InMemoryGrants` for an equivalent grant; every earlier suite unchanged and passing).

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **565 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 508); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165 (incl. `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`, re-run after the rebase), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** 22 mutants of `designation.py`/`access.py`/`documents.py` (OS evidence ignored, `note_os_granted` removed, freshness check removed or weakened, ungranted returns the grant, corrupt not caught, chain/head/state/grant-digest/schema checks removed, replay check removed, purge-pending guard removed, already-enabled no-op removed, decline-of-enabled guard removed, expiry removed, ceiling raised, scan/each-document/search/`get()` gates removed). Two survived at first (the chain hash itself, and the `get()` gate, because purge had already emptied the index); I added tamper cases that only the hash catches and a test where rows still exist but the OS evidence is not current, and both now fail as they should.

**Known limitations and pending evidence (not claimed):**
- Who may call the mutators (`apply`, `withdraw`) over the wire (an enrolled owner-interactive client, signed, replay-protected) is the consent-route package behind 1.1; here they are Core-internal and the architecture test keeps the edge from importing them. A single-use request id is replay protection, not client authentication.
- Expiry is enforced by a sweep on use; a denial caused by expiry therefore reports `grant_revoked` (closed reason enumeration unchanged), with `grant_expired` in the audit row.
- The store's key provider for real data is WP3.6 (tests use a test-only key). The real macOS permission provider, Get Started UI, TCC/FileVault behaviour and everything else Mac-only remain pending.
- Mail enablement can now be *recorded* (default-deny, independent), but nothing reads Mail; that is WP3.3 after this mechanism is accepted.

**Rollback:** delete `designation.py`, `access.py`, `test_designation.py`, `G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`; revert the `access` parameter/gate calls in `documents.py` and the layer/allow-list lines in two test files. The store is its own file.

## G-O19 corrections for review 6001015567 — one focused commit

**Commit: `c8da9b3fd05b3b69b00d4875bd495961dcfeb811`** (parent `dcb0fe24c0cf05b9310da6f12d4b736b38532bfa`; no concurrent remote commits this time; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. No Mail data touched; API 1.0 frozen, 1.1 unserved; no new dependency; no `docs/genesis` edits.

**Changed paths (all `core/`):** `src/atlas_core/designation.py`, `src/atlas_core/documents.py`, `tests/test_designation.py` (57 → 78 tests), `docs/G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`, `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`.

**Blocker 1 — whole-database rollback: fixed (with an honest boundary)**
- `DesignationStore` now requires a `RollbackAnchor` (keyword, no default): a tiny monotonic (sequence, head hash) record kept **outside** the database, advanced after every commit, never backwards (a lower sequence or a different head for the same sequence is refused). At every verification the database must contain the anchored sequence with the anchored hash. Refused (fail closed, no grant, no change): an older database (including one that only loses the last change), a used store with no anchor, a fork at the same sequence, an anchor that cannot be read. A database merely **ahead** of the anchor (crash between commit and anchor write, or an unavailable anchor) is safe and the anchor is caught up. A new empty store under an older anchor is refused until `reset_anchor()`, which works only on an empty store.
- **Boundary stated in code docs and `G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`:** in CI the anchor is `InMemoryAnchor`, a deterministic fake that only models the contract. The real platform secure-storage (Keychain) anchor is **not implemented or verified here (Mac-pending, WP3.6), so full rollback detection is NOT claimed** until it is. Also stated as outside the design: an attacker who can rewrite the anchor, and a crash exactly between a commit and its anchor write followed by a restore of the immediately preceding snapshot before the next open. The anchor is read at every verification; its real cost is unmeasured.
- Tests: the regression you specified (snapshot the complete database while enabled, withdraw, close, restore the old complete snapshot, reopen: `health == corrupt`, no grant returned, `permitted` false, changes refused), a snapshot that loses only the last change, missing anchor, unreadable anchor (recovers when readable), same-sequence/other-head, replaced empty database + `reset_anchor` (and refused on a non-empty store), monotonic anchor, and database-ahead-of-anchor for both a crash after commit and an unavailable anchor.

**Blocker 2 — withdrawal in two transactions: fixed**
- `withdraw()` now consumes the request id **and** revokes the grant (state, audit row, `purge_pending=1`) in one transaction; the purge runs only after that commit. Fault injection at `before_commit` and `after_commit`: a crash before the commit leaves the source enabled with the request id **unused** (the same request then succeeds); a crash after leaves a whole withdrawal with the purge still owed (finished by `run_pending_purges`). A parametrised test asserts "request consumed if and only if revoked" at both points.

**Blocker 3 — read after revocation: fixed**
- `Scanner` can no longer be constructed without an access guard (`TypeError` test). The guard runs **immediately before every `os.open` of a directory, every `scandir` listing and the file open, after any hook/seam**: at the start of `_open_dir`, after the per-component seam and before each component open, before the listing, and in `read()` right before the descriptor-relative file open. `DocumentIndex` passes its own `_require`, so scanning, indexing, listing and reading all go through it. A guard refusal during `get()` reads returns nothing instead of raising.
- Tests count the real `os.open` / `scandir` calls: consent withdrawn **and** OS permission lost at `doc:before` → **zero** opens (not "up to one"), nothing indexed, source revoked; access ending at `("before","sub")` mid-walk, at `("after","sub")` before the listing (only the root was listed), and just before the file open → the file is never opened; withdrawal at `scan:begin` → zero opens and never `complete`. The earlier test that allowed one read is tightened.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **586 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 565); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165 (incl. `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the full matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** removing the anchor comparison → 4 fail; dropping the anchored-hash condition → 1; removing "no anchor on a used store" → 1; swallowing an unreadable anchor → 1; removing the catch-up → 2; not advancing the anchor after commit → 2; making withdrawal not revoke → 18; allowing `reset_anchor` on a non-empty store → 1; removing the guard at root open → 3, between components → 1, before the file open → 1. Two survived at first: the guard immediately before the listing and the `get()` handling of a refused guard. I added a test for each (listing after a directory opened; access flipping between `get()`'s own check and the read) and both now fail as they should. One further mutant (dropping only the `seq > len(rows)` clause) is equivalent: the resulting `IndexError` is already treated as a corrupt record.

**Limits still stated:** the platform anchor, the real OS permission provider, Get Started UI, the store key provider (WP3.6) and the over-the-wire owner-client enforcement (consent-route package) are not built or verified here.

**Rollback:** revert this commit.

## Comment 7055024802 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## G-O19 second correction for review 6001660112 — one focused commit

**Commit: `19c38a11a270bcf424b31385f87da17f5446a28d`** (parent `c8da9b3fd05b3b69b00d4875bd495961dcfeb811`; no concurrent remote commits; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. No Mail data touched; API 1.0 frozen, 1.1 unserved; no new dependency; no `docs/genesis` edits.

**Changed paths (all `core/`):** `src/atlas_core/designation.py`, `tests/test_designation.py` (78 → 90 tests), `docs/G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`, `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`.

**You were right.** `_pin()` swallowed anchor failures, so a database ahead of the anchor stayed usable. I removed that tolerance and replaced the single-write anchor with a **two-phase protocol**:

1. **`prepare`** records the intended (sequence, head) in the external anchor **before** the database commits. If the anchor cannot record it, the change is **not made** (rolled back, nothing granted, nothing consumed).
2. The database commits.
3. **`commit`** records the finish in the anchor. If that cannot be recorded, **nothing is trusted**: at every verification a pending record that matches the database must first be finalised; if the anchor refuses, `grants_for` returns no grant, `permitted` is false, and every change raises `StoreCorrupt("anchor_unsynced")` until the anchor is reconciled. This covers reads and writes, including right after a committed enable or withdrawal. A withdrawal that committed while the anchor refused the finish still runs its purge (it does not depend on the verification).
4. The database is never accepted ahead of what the anchor has recorded (`unanchored` and `pending_mismatch` fail closed); it is never accepted older than the anchor (`rollback`), missing an anchor on a used store, or against an unreadable anchor.

**The crash/restore window is closed by recorded intent, not just documented.** If the anchor holds a pending change the database lacks (process died before the commit, or an older snapshot was restored over a database whose finish the anchor never recorded), the record decides: a **restriction** (owner withdrawal, expiry, loss of the OS permission; carried as an `Intent` with source type, reason code and request id) is **applied now** to the database and then recorded, so a withdrawal can never be undone by a crash or by restoring an older snapshot; an **ordinary change** (enable, decline) is **dropped**, so nothing is ever granted by a change that did not commit. The retried request id of a completed withdrawal then reports `replayed`. An in-process failure (an ordinary exception) still aborts the pending record, so the earlier atomicity guarantees (enabled with the request unused, retryable) hold.

**Tests required by the review (all in `test_designation.py`):**
- With `fail_commit=True`, after a committed **enable** and after a committed **withdrawal**: no grant returned, `permitted` false, health `corrupt`, further changes refused while the anchor stays behind; after the anchor recovers the committed state is trusted (and the withdrawal's purge had already run).
- With `fail_prepare=True`: enable and withdrawal raise and change nothing (audit unchanged, request id unused).
- The scenario in your note: snapshot while enabled, withdraw with the anchor unable to finish (and, separately, process death after the commit), restore the old complete snapshot, reopen → the withdrawal is applied to the restored database; no grant, `permitted` false (and with the anchor still refusing, nothing is trusted). The same for an unfinished enable: restored, nothing granted.
- Process death at `before_commit` for enable (dropped, store clean), for withdrawal (completed at next start, request id consumed, purge run), for automatic revocation by OS-permission loss and by expiry (completed at next start); process death at `after_commit` (finalised at next start).
- Anchor contract tests (forward-only, one unresolved change at a time, finish must equal intent, replacement only by the same restriction); an unrecorded database change; a pending record matching neither state. The earlier snapshot, missing-anchor, unreadable-anchor and replaced-database tests are kept.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **598 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 586); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165, `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the full matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** not finalising a matching pending record → 3 fail; accepting a database ahead of the anchor → 1; dropping the ambiguity handling → 4; the pending-mismatch refusal → 1; treating restrictions as droppable → 3; skipping `prepare` → 68; skipping the finish → 3; not aborting after an ordinary failure → 1; not replaying the request id → 1; not running the purge after roll-forward → 1; weakening the anchored-hash comparison → 1; withdrawal without its intent → 3; first-store anchor initialisation removed → 75. One survived at first (the intent for automatic revocations: OS loss and expiry); I added the crash test for both and it now fails as it should.

**Acceptance boundary (stated in `G_O19_DESIGNATION.md` and `THREAT_REVIEW.md`; please read it as the limit of the claim):** the real platform secure-storage anchor (Keychain) is **not implemented or verified here (Mac-pending, WP3.6)**; CI uses `InMemoryAnchor`, a deterministic fake that models this contract. The design is rollback-resistant only **relative to the integrity of that anchor**; **full rollback detection is not claimed until the platform anchor is verified.** An attacker who can forge or delete-and-replace the anchor is outside what it detects (a deleted anchor on a used store is refused; a forged one is not distinguishable). The anchor is read at every verification; its real cost is unmeasured. Over-the-wire owner-client enforcement, the real OS permission provider, Get Started UI and the store key provider remain pending, as before.

**Rollback:** revert this commit.

Standing by for your exact-SHA re-review of `19c38a1`.

## Comment 7064311326 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## G-O19 third correction for review 6006012296 — one focused commit

**Commit: `8046df340b5916e9ac07d88b9e12552740423f64`** (parent `19c38a11a270bcf424b31385f87da17f5446a28d`; no concurrent remote commits; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. No Mail data touched; API 1.0 frozen, 1.1 unserved; no new dependency; no `docs/genesis` edits.

**Changed paths (all `core/`):** `src/atlas_core/designation.py`, `tests/test_designation.py` (90 → 109 tests), `docs/G_O19_DESIGNATION.md`, `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`.

**Principle applied:** nothing security-relevant lives outside the audited, anchored record. I removed the mutable columns `os_seen`, `purge_pending` and `revoke_reason` (schema version 2) and made both facts audit events that go through the same two-phase anchored transaction as every other change.

**Finding 1 — `os_seen` outside the integrity boundary: fixed**
- "The platform permission was observed for this grant" is now the audit event `os_permission_seen` (once per grant, written by `note_os_granted()` through `_transaction`, so it is hash-chained and anchored). `os_was_granted()` is derived from the chain joined to the verified state. There is no column to roll back.
- Your regression test, as specified: enable, observe fresh positive OS permission (audited), snapshot the database, revoke the OS permission and complete the revocation and purge (anchored), restore the snapshot **preserving the external anchor**, OS permission returns → `health == corrupt`, no grant returned, `permitted` false, lifecycle `not_configured`. The old consent never becomes effective. A second test covers the snapshot from **before** the permission was ever seen (it would otherwise hide a later loss).
- Direct mutation: editing the recorded observation's reason, or deleting the row, breaks the chain/head → corrupt; a forged **correctly hashed** observation is refused both without the anchor (`unanchored`) and with it, when it is a second observation for the grant (see legality rules below).

**Finding 2 — `purge_pending` not integrity-checked: fixed**
- "Purge completed" is now the audit event `purge_completed`, recorded after the purge function ran. "Purge owed" is **derived from the chain** (a revoked grant with no such event), never read from a flag. `_enable` and the chain rules refuse a new grant until the old grant's completion is recorded.
- Running a purge is not the same as recording it: a purge whose completion cannot be recorded (anchor refuses) or whose process died first stays owed, blocks a new grant (`PurgePending`), and is simply run again (tests: platform store dies right after the purge; process death inside the purge; recovery then allows the new grant).
- Restore/tamper tests: an older snapshot in which the purge is still owed, restored under the advanced anchor → corrupt (the purge is neither shown as owed nor as done; nothing is trusted); a forged `purge_completed` without the anchor → corrupt and a new grant refused.

**Chain legality (new, closes the "correctly hashed forgery" class):** at every verification each event must be a legal step from the state the chain has reached: an observation only for the currently enabled grant and only once; a purge completion only for a revoked grant and only once; a new grant only from a state whose old grant's purge is recorded; a revocation only of the enabled grant; a decline only from a non-enabled state. Tests forge events with valid hashes **and** a moved anchor (an attacker holding both the key and the anchor) for: a purge completion on an enabled grant, a duplicate observation, an enable from `not_configured`, a revoke of a grant the chain does not show enabled, a duplicate purge completion, and a fully consistent forged enable (grant row, state row, audit digest, hash, anchor all agreeing) over an owed purge. All are refused by the chain rules alone.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **617 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 598); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165, `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the full matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** removing the legality check → 4 fail; allowing a duplicate observation → 2; allowing a duplicate purge completion → survived at first, now 1 (added the forged duplicate case); not recording the observation → 9; not recording the purge → 23; `_purge_owed` always false → 3; not recording after a successful purge → 23; treating any `to_state == enabled` row as an issuance → 37; allowing an enable over an owed purge → survived at first because the table checks also reject my crude forgery, so I added the fully consistent forgery above and it now fails as it should.

**Acceptance boundary, unchanged and stated in the docs:** the real platform anchor (Keychain) is Mac-pending and CI uses `InMemoryAnchor`; the design is rollback-resistant only relative to the integrity of that anchor; full rollback detection is not claimed. An attacker who also controls the anchor can append legal-looking events (for example a `purge_completed`) that the chain rules accept; that is outside what this design detects and is now stated explicitly. Over-the-wire owner-client enforcement, the real OS permission provider, Get Started UI and the store key provider remain pending.

**Rollback:** revert this commit.

## Comment 7069795504 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## Architect brief — begin WP3.3 Mail read-only path (synthetic-only; no owner Mail access)

**Gate update:** G-O19's Mac-independent designation/consent mechanism is accepted at exact head `8046df340b5916e9ac07d88b9e12552740423f64` (review 6008436774). The three prior WP3.3 security findings are accepted at that same SHA. CI is 22/22. This authorises the next plan-order package, but it does NOT mean Mail has been enabled by the owner or that macOS Keychain/TCC is implemented.

### Authorised scope now
Implement the WP3.3 **read-only Mail body path against synthetic fixtures only**, behind the existing Core policy and source-access interfaces.

1. Keep `mail` an independent source type. A Documents grant must never imply Mail access.
2. Require current owner grant AND current OS permission evidence before every Mail metadata/body read. Use the existing `SourceAccess` / `AccessCheck` boundary; do not add a parallel permission system or accept caller-supplied booleans.
3. Preserve trusted stored message identity, version/integrity metadata, stored sender/date/header fields and exact body-span locators through retrieval and citations. Never infer sender/date from message text.
4. Treat Mail body content as untrusted data. Add hostile prompt-injection fixtures requesting broader search, secret disclosure, sending, permission changes or policy overrides; prove none can change scope or trigger actions.
5. Add synthetic tests for default-deny, independent grants, OS permission missing/unknown/stale/provider failure, revocation during retrieval, revoke/regrant before model use, stale/changed messages, malformed records, redaction, restart/idempotency and exact citations.
6. Keep attachment content entirely unavailable. Do not enumerate, open, parse, index or expose attachment bytes/metadata as content. G-O18 remains a separate package.
7. Preserve API 1.0 byte-frozen and API 1.1 specification-only/unserved. No cloud inference, no private content in CI/logs, no new dependency without an explicit justification.

### Explicitly prohibited in this package
- Do not open, inspect, copy, query or ingest the owner's real Apple Mail store or any real mailbox.
- Do not enable Mail on the owner's behalf.
- Do not claim macOS TCC or Keychain behaviour is verified. Use deterministic fakes in CI and retain the platform seam; actual Keychain anchor/TCC provider and owner-authorised end-to-end validation remain gated on target Mac and explicit owner enablement.
- No Mail attachments.

### Delivery and acceptance
Work from exact base `8046df340b5916e9ac07d88b9e12552740423f64`; one bounded WP3.3 commit/PR update. Report changed paths, threat/coverage mapping, test commands/results, CI run, known limitations and exact SHA. Do not start WP3.4 or broaden scope. I will independently review the exact SHA before any next package.

**Threat/coverage mapping to the brief (details in `core/docs/WP3_3_MAIL.md`)**
1. **Independent source type.** `MailIndex` is a `Retriever`/`SourceStore` for the unchanged `TaskService` and names no source type in code. Tests with the real `SourceAccess` over the designation store: documents-only, mail-only, neither, both. A Documents grant never implies Mail (mail denied `no_grant`, adapter untouched).
2. **Both gates before every adapter call, no parallel permission system.** The index keeps only a guarded handle on the adapter: every `list` and `read` first runs `SourceAccess.permitted` (owner grant AND fresh positive OS evidence, now). It cannot be built without an access check. Tests count real adapter calls: nothing enabled; OS evidence missing/unknown/stale/future-dated/provider error; consent withdrawn and OS permission lost at `scan:begin` and at `msg:before` → **zero** listings/reads, the owner's consent untouched on a mere "unknown", a denied scan changes no index state, and mail already in local storage is unretrievable once access ends even if the purge has not run.
3. **Trusted stored fields, exact locators.** Sender, date and subject are the stored fields, rendered by the Core as a separate `headers` part (one passage per line, `message_part: headers`, exact span); the body is a separate `body` part (one passage per paragraph, `message_part: body`, exact span); version = hash of the stored text. Sender/date are never parsed from message text: a body containing `From: ceo@evil...` / a forged `Date:` is only ever a **body** citation, and claiming it is a header does not resolve (`out_of_range`). Uses only the frozen 1.0 `EvidenceRef`/`message_part` locator. Stated limit: "stored sender" is the claimed header Mail stored, not an authenticated identity.
4. **Hostile content is data.** End-to-end fixtures ask to search every mailbox and the documents folder, reveal secrets, forward to an attacker, delete, grant access to Finance/Health and change policy: only the granted type is searched, the question reaches the model unchanged, no grant appears or changes (documents stays `not_configured`), citations are mail only, no event beyond the task's own, and a request naming documents still gets documents `not_searched`. There is no action path in the module.
5. **Synthetic test list covered:** default deny; independent grants; OS missing/unknown/stale/future/provider failure; revocation during retrieval (before search and during the model call: nothing served, index purged); revoke/regrant between retrieval and recovery (no old passage reused, model never called); stale/changed/deleted messages (old citations stop resolving: `stale`/`unresolvable`; a message changed while the model answers is not verified); malformed records (missing/mistyped fields, unparseable date, a header line smuggled through a sender, control characters, NUL, oversized subject/body, a record for another message, an invalid identity: each an explicit reason-coded failure, never repaired, never searchable, blocking `complete`); redaction (no sender, subject, body or identity in logs, coverage or the raw encrypted index file); restart/idempotency (a six-boundary crash matrix converges to the clean-run index; re-running does not re-read unchanged messages; cancellation; bounded slices); exact citations (every passage resolves to its exact text; an answer cites the exact body span).
6. **Attachments entirely unavailable.** Only five fields are ever taken from the adapter; anything else (`attachments`, extra headers) is ignored and never stored, listed or counted: tested with a leaky adapter, with a fixture that counts any attachment access (zero), and by asserting there is not even a column for them. The answer discloses `attachments_not_searched` and the deferred `mail_attachment` source via the existing rule. G-O18 is untouched.
7. **No private content in CI/logs.** Invented sentinels only; sentinel scans of logs, coverage and the raw index file.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **670 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 617); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165 (incl. `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the full matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** removing the guard on `list` → 1 fail, on `read` → 2; removing the scan-start gate → 1 (after I added the "a denied scan changes no state" assertion; it survived before); removing the search gate → 3; control-character, NUL, oversize and wrong-record checks → 2/1/1/1; skipping the re-hash on resolution → 2; labelling body passages as headers → 3; ignoring the change marker → 1; accepting invalid identities → 1; completing despite failures → 9. Equivalent mutants, stated honestly: the per-message gate inside the run loop (the guarded read already refuses; I removed the redundant call) and the early `get()` gate (externally identical; kept as defence in depth so no stored text is read after access ends).

**Known limitations and pending evidence (not claimed):** synthetic fixtures only; the real Apple Mail adapter, its message identity/versioning, scale and performance are unverified; thread context, recipients/other headers, HTML/MIME and a disk-pressure pause are not built; coverage is produced and tested but not yet in the answer envelope (1.1); ranking is lexical; the listing change marker is adapter-supplied and only an optimisation (correctness never depends on it). Mac-pending: TCC/Mail automation permission, the Keychain anchor and store key provider, owner-authorised end-to-end validation (which needs explicit owner enablement).

## Comment 7070475371 — Prasanna666666 — timestamp unavailable

## WP3.3 corrections for review 6008667627 — one focused commit

**Commit: `aa9f9a1f32ce7da1227c38dceea7a2b923568600`** (parent `d82140c150367302bc2cea66e000dcd7d6cfc2eb`; no concurrent remote commits; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged. I will not push while this commit awaits review. WP3.4 not started. Synthetic fixtures only; no real mailbox, no attachments; API 1.0 frozen, 1.1 unserved; no new dependency; no `docs/genesis` edits.

**Changed paths (all `core/`):** `src/atlas_core/mail.py`, `src/atlas_core/documents.py` (shared `IndexStore`), `tests/test_mail.py` (53 → 66), `tests/test_designation.py` (+2 document-index tests), `docs/WP3_3_MAIL.md`, `docs/THREAT_REVIEW.md`, `docs/TEST_PLAN.md`, `README.md`.

**Blocker 1 — revocation during a listing can repopulate the index after purge: fixed**
- Every index write now re-checks access **inside its own transaction**: `IndexStore._transaction` runs the access check while the index lock is held and immediately before `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. So material listed or read while permitted can never be written after a revocation: a concurrent purge either runs after the write and clears it (the purge needs the same lock), or a completed revocation makes the check fail and nothing is written. The purge is the only unguarded write (`guarded=False`), so a revocation can always complete.
- The slow adapter listing now runs **outside** the index lock (previously `scan()` held it across `list()`), so a revocation and its purge are not blocked by a slow adapter. The scan-start marker, the scan apply, the pause markers and every per-message store are guarded writes; generation is read inside the apply transaction.
- Your deterministic test, as specified: a `Blocking` adapter; scan begins while permitted, blocks inside `list_page`; the owner withdraws consent and the purge **completes** (asserted: no pending purge, index empty); the listing is released; the scan raises `PermissionRevoked`; **no message row and no scan metadata** exist, **no body was read** (`reads == 0`). The OS-permission-loss variant: the platform permission is seen then reported missing while the listing is in flight; on release the guarded write revokes, purges and raises; index empty, zero reads. Also: revocation between two listing pages (the next page is never requested, index empty) and between a read and its write (`msg:extracted`: one permitted read, nothing stored).
- **The document index had the same window** (walk then write; read then write), so it gets the same guarded write, with two regression tests (revocation between a walk and its write, and between a read and its write: index empty).

**Blocker 2 — the cap did not bound listing and was not newest-first: fixed**
- The adapter seam is now **`list_page(cursor, limit)`** returning a bounded page plus a cursor (no materialised full list). `PAGE_SIZE` bounds each call, and a page larger than asked is cut to the limit **before anything is walked** (a test counts entries iterated: zero).
- Selection: the Core admits at most `MAX_MESSAGES` entries to a scan, the **newest by stored date**, using a min-heap whose memory is bounded by the cap, and examines at most `MAX_LIST_WORK` entries whatever the adapter does. If the adapter lists newest first (the stated contract) consumption stops as soon as enough are held; the Core **verifies that order as it goes** and, if it is ever broken, keeps reading (to the work bound) while retaining only the newest. Missing/unparseable dates sort as oldest. A mailbox not fully admitted (more pages left, an oversized page, or more entries than the cap) is reported: `scan_complete` false and never `complete`.
- Tests (cap/page/work shrunk by monkeypatch): 100 messages in date order → exactly the five newest admitted using two pages (`listed ≤ 6`, `max_page ≤ 3`); out-of-date-order listings (by id, and a deliberately reversed insertion order) → bounded work (`listed ≤ 30`, `max_page ≤ 3`) and exactly the newest of what was examined; a 500-message oldest-first mailbox (the worst case for a "first N" cap) → 30 entries examined, 3 at a time, five admitted, never complete; a page larger than asked; a mailbox that fits → listed whole and can be complete; and the newest are indexed first after a capped scan.

**Tests (developer-reported, Linux container):** core **685 passed** on Python 3.13 and 3.11 (was 670); ruff format/check and mypy clean; `kit` 165 (incl. `kit/tests/test_architecture.py`), `images` 100, `library` 299, `atlas` 111, `finance` 701 passed. CI on this head: just pushed; I will report the full matrix separately, not claimed here.

**Mutation checks (exact-string, each restored):** removing the in-write guard → 4 fail; unsetting the mail write guard → 3; dropping the ordered early-stop → 1; dropping the work bound → 1; not bounding the heap → 4; not cutting an oversized page → survived at first (the heap bounded memory anyway), so I added a test that counts entries the Core iterates and it now fails; reversing the returned order → survived at first (the index orders by date itself), so I added a direct order assertion and it now fails; not reporting an incomplete listing → 5; trusting the adapter's order → 1; ignoring an oversized page → 1; holding the index lock across the listing → 1.

**Limits still stated:** synthetic fixtures only; the listing bounds assume only that the adapter honours `limit` and the cursor (an adapter that never ends its cursor is stopped by the work bound, one that ignores the limit is cut to it); whether a real Apple Mail adapter can page newest-first efficiently is unverified (Mac-pending); the stored sender is a claimed header, not an authenticated identity; thread context, HTML/MIME, a disk-pressure pause and the Mac-only items remain unbuilt or unverified.

**Rollback:** revert this commit.

**Commit: `a7a32ef1e92b85f4eae43422d3c56121c10402c8`** (parent `aa9f9a1f32ce7da1227c38dceea7a2b923568600`; no concurrent remote commits; origin head verified). PR #8 only, draft, unmerged.
